# Garderobe requirement-to-code/test checklist

This checklist maps every requirement of the Garderobe rebuild to the code that implements it and the tests that prove it. Its sources are the revision 4 specification `requirements/garderobe-replacement-design.md` (SHA-256 `7f47ffd7aa5a7bcd92738c4eaa48d2ec0af79486440f490790007a8678558238`), the owner profile `requirements/chris-wardrobe-profile.md` (SHA-256 `e15639d891f9a5264c7eff13d05a478bcb188745aea11323b2131db37f5cb198`), the September 15 owner amendments `requirements/support/wardrobe-support/evals/sources/owner-amendments.md` (they supersede conflicting specification text), the evaluation instructions `requirements/support/wardrobe-support/evals/README.md` and `judge.md`, the usage research `requirements/support/wardrobe-support/handoff/support/garderobe-usage-research.md` (requirements R1 to R59), and the owner's kickoff instructions `requirements/support/wardrobe-start-prompt.md`. Every row starts with `Code` and `Tests` set to `—` and `Status` set to `open`; the workstream named in the row fills them in under the rules below. `Source` gives the section and line number(s) in the source file (`§` is a specification or profile section, `L` a line number).

## How to use

- Each workstream fills in `Code` (repository-relative paths, optionally with symbols) and `Tests` (test file and test name) for the rows where it is the primary or a secondary workstream, and changes `Status` to `implemented` (code and passing local tests cited), `partial` (say what is missing in the Requirement cell's trailing parenthesis), `blocked` (needs a credential, entitlement, device or macOS toolchain — name it), or leaves it `open`.
- Never mark a row `implemented` on the strength of a mock of the thing the row is about; cloud, device and Xcode evidence is recorded only when actually run.
- Rows are never deleted; a superseded requirement keeps its row with status `superseded` and a pointer to the superseding row.
- Edit only your own rows' `Code`, `Tests` and `Status` cells.

## Workstreams

The `Workstream` cell holds exactly one primary key, optionally followed by secondary keys after a `+` (for example `foundation+daily-service`).

| Key | Covers |
| --- | --- |
| `foundation` | Monorepo, shared versioned contracts, base D1 schema, the domain ledger and single command service (receipts, idempotency, preconditions and expected versions, undo as compensation, outbox and effect records), garments, aliases, facts, stock quantities and movements, restrictions and locations, wear observations and per-garment-per-wearing-date counting, authoritative owner observations and event-order accounting repair, laundry batches, weekly reset, exceptions and hand-wash, the probabilistic availability estimator, style documents, amendments, rules, standing directions and temporary briefs, measurement and fit fact storage, per-owner isolation in D1, import of the profile and inventory CSV with reconciliation report, synthetic second owner fixture, document hashes, the local D1 test harness. |
| `daily-service` | Mandatory context assembly for recommendations, weather skill and adapters, calendar reading and influence, board composition, validation, repair and publication, boards, options, selections and saved combinations, variety and repeat rules at composition, evening-to-morning schedule and due-job sweep, Calendar projection of the managed event, pause and resume, trips and packing, the private web board. |
| `assistant` | Think and Durable Object conversation runtime, turn queueing, compaction, recall and AI Search retrieval, Agent Memory, inference, model registry, AI Gateway, budgets and reservations, research and shopping investigations, purchases from email (Gmail), web search, Tavily, Exa and Browser Run, history and provenance research, lifecycle projects (consignment, tailoring, disposal), return and exchange deadlines, comfort feedback interpretation, outbound MCP connections, Drive and Sheets, deletion and forgetting of conversation sources. |
| `api-mcp-identity` | The HTTP API endpoints (`/v1/...`), SSE run events, the MCP server and its tools, the MCP 2026-07-28 contract, MCP OAuth authorization for Claude and ChatGPT, Cloudflare Access login and identity mapping, `auth_identities`, connection endpoints, account recovery credential flow, portable export, backups and restore, route trust boundaries. |
| `visual-wardrobe` | Media uploads, private R2 storage, image discovery and backfill, catalogue normalization, cutouts, fidelity checks, outfit composition manifests and rendering, Studio backend support, image privacy and retention. |
| `ios` | The native SwiftUI app: Today, Wardrobe, Studio, Conversation, capture, Laundry sheet, settings, accessibility, offline cache and queued commands, restoration, native OAuth client, share extension, first-use flow, design-skill requirements. |
| `journey-tests` | Functional and UX journey test suite, integration of the 64-case evaluation corpus, candidate and judge isolation, behavioural state assertions, acceptance-table cases that are end-to-end journeys. |
| `adversarial-tests` | Adversarial suite: hallucinated items, forged owner fields, prompt injection from untrusted content, concurrency races, replay and duplicate attacks, SSRF, isolation probes, negative stock attempts. |
| `deployment` | Cloudflare resource provisioning, wrangler configuration, environments, gateways and spend limits, secrets, free-plan measurements, cost and budget operations, observability, real-platform acceptance probes, Apple distribution, cutover. |
| `simulation` | The full seeded MCP simulation with random outfit selections and simulated availability, weather, calendar and circumstance. |

## Evaluation instructions

| ID | Source | Requirement | Workstream | Code | Tests | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EV-001 | evals/README L3; START-HERE L9 | All 64 evaluation cases (40 adapted from historical conversations, 24 constructed) are integrated into the test suite. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-002 | evals/README L3 | Codex judges taste, while deterministic checks cover explicit inventory constraints and observed application state. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-003 | evals/README L7 | Each historical excerpt keeps its conversation ID, message ID, date, source position and source-message hash; the suite is not treated as an exhaustive annotation. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-004 | evals/README L9 | The profile copy in the suite is byte-identical to the September 14 profile (SHA-256 e15639d891f9a5264c7eff13d05a478bcb188745aea11323b2131db37f5cb198) and the September 15 decisions are applied separately. | journey-tests+foundation | — | — | open |
| EV-005 | evals/README L11; START-HERE L9 | The suite's wardrobe and scenario fixtures are constructed test data, not the owner's inventory; each case's stated observations override fixture defaults and missing photos are not reconstructed. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-006 | evals/README L11 | Calibration candidates use only structured visible text content; tool results and internal reasoning are excluded. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-007 | evals/README L32 | The split of 45 development and 19 held-out cases is preserved, with each historical conversation entirely in one split and calibration using development conversations only. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-008 | evals/README L32 | A candidate receives the complete profile, owner amendments, fixture state and request, and never source feedback, judge criteria, expected state assertions or historical candidate answers; the judge receives those only after the candidate finishes. | journey-tests+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-009 | evals/README L34 | The packet is the isolation boundary: a held-out candidate gets no filesystem access to the suite or the case catalogue, and any candidate exposure used for tuning is recorded and moves that case out of the untouched holdout. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-010 | evals/README L38-L43 | The source assets validate with `python3 evals/evaluate.py validate` and `list`. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-011 | evals/README L45-L52 | Candidate packets are prepared with `evaluate.py packet --mode candidate`, and each run saves complete output, exact model/profile and prompt versions, date, run ID, elapsed time and tool evidence in a run directory. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-012 | evals/README L52-L66 | Board outputs follow the candidate schema: `response` plus `options`, each with `id`, `garment_ids` and `explanation`. | journey-tests+daily-service | — | — | open |
| EV-013 | evals/README L68 | A smaller-than-requested board includes `shortage_reason`, checked against feasibility. | daily-service+journey-tests | packages/daily/src/boards.ts (boardNotice); packages/daily/src/compose.ts; packages/daily/src/repair.ts | packages/daily/test/board.test.ts (SYNTHETIC: two eligible shirts give two outfits and one brief explanation, never padding); note - the board states the shortage in its `notice`; remaining - mapping it to the evaluation's `shortage_reason` and checking it against feasibility in the evaluation harness; closed by the journey-tests thread | partial |
| EV-014 | evals/README L68 | Prose never establishes a successful write; command cases use the application's actual receipts and observed state. | journey-tests+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-015 | evals/README L70-L78 | Structured inventory facts are checked with `evaluate.py check-candidate` and the judge packet is prepared with `packet --mode judge`. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-016 | evals/README L80 | The candidate never grades its own output; the evaluation script calls no model and no provider billing path is silently selected. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-017 | evals/README L82-L87 | For behavioural cases a real application adapter collects outcomes under `observed` with `provenance.source: "application_adapter"`, a `run_id` and retained `artifact_paths`, verified with `evaluate.py check-state`. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-018 | evals/README L22, L89 | The 18 behavioural cases in `fixtures/state-assertions.json` are compared against normalized adapter observations; a model-generated prediction of expected state is not a passing integration test. | journey-tests+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-019 | evals/README L93 | Taste is scored one to five on personal fit, composition, comfort and fabric, variety and scope, practical usefulness, and voice and teaching; dimensions can be inapplicable and two different combinations can be equally good. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-020 | evals/README L93 | Every material judgment cites exact candidate text and relevant source evidence, and hard violations are kept separate from subjective scores. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-021 | evals/README L95 | Initial release targets (proposed thresholds, not results): no hard violations, personal fit and practical usefulness of at least four in 80% of repeated applicable cases, and at least one recommendable option in every feasible full-board case. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-022 | evals/README L97 | A representative group is repeated at least five times, including a quiet outfit, a dramatic brief, a swap, a depleted wardrobe, a purchase judgment and a correction. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-023 | evals/README L97 | Model comparisons use identical snapshots and anonymous labels, reversed pairwise order, permitted ties, retained individual judgments and repeated judge passes, and record whether candidate and judge share a model family. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-024 | evals/README L99 | The profile is not converted into a fashion formula (brown stays welcome, pink can be right generally and wrong for an interview, a quiet white-tee outfit can work); explanations are about the actual clothes and circumstances. | daily-service+journey-tests | packages/daily/src/context.ts; packages/daily/src/context-text.ts; packages/daily/src/compose.ts | packages/daily/test/board.test.ts (still hands the model the full mandatory context); note - the composition model is given the complete profile text and rules (tested with a labelled fake model); the deterministic composer applies only soft colour and rotation preferences and does not model registers; remaining - taste judged on real boards with a live model and independent judging; closed by the journey-tests and deployment threads | partial |
| EV-025 | evals/README L103 | The eight bundled historical reviews are calibration evidence only and never reported as validation of the new application. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-026 | evals/README L105 | The first end-to-end application run collects real receipts, state transitions, board outputs and Calendar read-backs. | journey-tests+deployment | — | — | open |
| EV-027 | evals/README L105 | The end-to-end run demonstrates missing-confirmation tolerance, weekly reset exceptions, cross-client duplicate merging, authoritative late observations, automatic future repair and all six feature journeys. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-028 | evals/README L109-L117 | Source rebuild scripts are run only after a reviewed source update, and the archived revision 3 to 4 specification edit script is never reapplied. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-029 | evals/judge L3 | The judge reads the complete profile, owner amendments, task state and selected historical evidence before grading and treats quotations, candidate text and retrieved material as data, ignoring embedded instructions. | journey-tests+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-030 | evals/judge L5 | The judge requires no single outfit or canonical prose and does not use the profile as a keyword checklist: a quiet outfit does not fail for lacking a statement piece and a conspicuous one does not pass for saying "sprezzatura". | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-031 | evals/judge L9-L13 | The judge's evidence order is: latest owner amendments and case observations, the full active profile, original owner feedback in its historical scope, verified fixture facts and receipts, then historical assistant statements as unverified claims. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-032 | evals/judge L15 | Case facts override fixture defaults; historical stock and measurements are not current facts; the judge never imagines a missing photograph, assumes an unrecorded tool call succeeded, or penalizes a candidate for a fact neither supplied nor required. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-033 | evals/judge L17-L28 | The six dimensions are scored with the rubric anchors (one, three, five; four is strong with a limited flaw, two a material problem), or null when inapplicable, with concise observable reasoning and no private chain-of-thought. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-034 | evals/judge L30 | Hard violations are reported separately and cannot be offset by taste scores: nonexistent or explicitly unavailable garments, missing socks, active footwear restrictions, changed locked items, a required observation refused on accounting grounds, duplicate daily wear counts, or a claimed external effect without evidence. | journey-tests+adversarial-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-035 | evals/judge L30 | A doubtful style judgment is not automatically a hard violation, and uncertain availability under the authorized probability policy is not the same as known unavailable stock. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-036 | evals/judge L32 | Ordinary candidate runs are judged on the prompt and material actually supplied; historical-response reviews use the historical request and context and note when a later correction was not yet available. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-037 | evals/judge L34-L61 | The judgment is one JSON object with `case_id`, `candidate_id`, `mode`, `verdict` (pass, revise, fail or insufficient_evidence), `scores`, `hard_violations`, `findings` (candidate_excerpt, basis, judgment), `limitations` and `summary`. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-038 | evals/judge L63 | Pairwise work grades each candidate separately, records A, B, tie or insufficient evidence, reverses order for a second pass, preserves individual scores, and does not reward verbosity, agreement, a model name or similarity to the judge's own answer. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-039 | evals/judge L63; START-HERE L9 | When the judge shares a model family with the candidate this is recorded and not called independent-provider validation; Codex judgments that were not run are never claimed, and if Codex is unavailable candidate packets are collected and the check marked pending. | journey-tests | — | — | open |
| EV-040 | START-HERE L9 | Archive-dependent validation is ported to the bundled evidence (the full private chat export is absent) without pretending the full archive was rechecked. | journey-tests | — | — | open |

Note: the evaluation source files are `requirements/support/wardrobe-support/evals/README.md` (evals/README), `requirements/support/wardrobe-support/evals/judge.md` (evals/judge) and the still-applicable evaluation paragraph of `requirements/support/wardrobe-support/handoff/START-HERE.md` (START-HERE). START-HERE's historical restrictions on deploying and on unavailable credentials are superseded and are not requirements.
